R-051 attempt on branch `reliability/weekend-2026-08-16-deferred`: no red/green was run and NO code was changed — after ~15 successful shell commands in the runner clone the harness began refusing EVERY shell execution (Bash foreground, background, and Monitor alike) with "session is isolated in worktree `.claude/worktrees/reliability-no-defer` … command's working directory resolved to the shared checkout", while EnterWorktree simultaneously reported the session sits at the repository root and is NOT worktree-isolated, so the demanded isolation could be neither entered, exited, nor satisfied. Root-cause hypothesis: the subagent's spawn cwd is pinned to the shared checkout while a concurrent session's worktree-isolation record (`reliability-no-defer`) got attached to this session mid-run; the pre-command guard validates the SESSION cwd, not the command's `cd` target, so no command text can pass it. Scope analysis for the next attempt: cut `CONTENT_HASH_TTL_MS` 300s→~15s (keep `CLIENT_KEY_TTL_MS` 300s); add a shared `placeOrderFeedback` helper (client-safe — must NOT import `orderIdempotency.ts`, which pulls `node:fs`) rendering `deduplicated:true` as a `warning`-token suppressed-submit state in ALL surfaces: OrderTab (2 call sites), OptionsChainTab, PositionTradeTicket, MobileOrderTicket + ListedContractOrderForm + SingleLegOrderTicket inline states, BookTab/InstrumentDetailModal `onSuccessToast` type plumb-through, chat.ts message. Two existing tests pin the indeterminate hold horizon to `CONTENT_HASH_TTL_MS` (`order-idempotency.test.ts` "holds an indeterminate key for the full durable content-hash TTL", `order-idempotency-durability.test.ts` "still honours the durable content-hash TTL after a restart") — with a TTL below the 60s `INDETERMINATE_RETENTION_MS` floor they must advance by `max(CONTENT_HASH_TTL_MS, INDETERMINATE_RETENTION_MS)` instead (same invariant, not a weakening). This row is left UNCOMMITTED because git itself is unreachable; next step should `git add RELIABILITY_LOG.md` and commit it before starting. |

(a) **R-117:** `reqHistoricalDataAsync` now carries the deadline and the outer `asyncio.wait_for` sits `HISTORICAL_CANCEL_GRACE_SECS` later. ib_insync's own `timeout` is the ONLY path that sends `cancelHistoricalData(reqId)`; the outer 15 s always fired first and merely cancelled the local coroutine, leaking one of IB's ~50 simultaneous historical slots per timeout on the long-lived pooled `data` client. ib_insync signals its timeout by returning an EMPTY container without raising, so the empty-and-slow case is distinguished from empty-and-fast (a contract with genuinely no bars) by elapsed time; a `keep_up_to_date` timeout additionally calls `cancelHistoricalData(bars)` because ib_insync's own path leaves the SUBSCRIPTION registered. `/historical/bars` and `/historical/head-timestamp` map `IBTimeoutError` to 503 — it subclasses `IBError`, not `ConnectionError`, so a slow gateway was an unhandled 500. `test_ib_event_waits.py::test_get_historical_data_times_out` stubbed a hang that ignored its own `timeout` kwarg and returned `None`, which no gateway does; it was rewritten onto ib_insync's real contract (same assertion) and joined by a case for the outer backstop. (b) **R-121:** `daily_needs_refresh` / `fetch_daily_if_stale` scrape StockCharts `$TRIN` only when the last closed session is missing. The 5-minute sampler scraped on all 108 daily cycles for at most one new row, against an endpoint the breadth scan shares. The timer comment claimed the opposite and was corrected. (c) **R-127:** `radon-credit-spread` and `radon-iei-hyg` share IB client IDs 56/69 on a documented "21:45 and 21:55 never overlap" invariant that `RandomizedDelaySec=300` on both timers falsifies. Both now serialize on `flock -w <peer work ceiling> -E 75 /run/lock/radon-ib-history-5669.lock` with `SuccessExitStatus=75`, the R-067 db-maintenance shape; each `TimeoutStartSec` absorbs its own wait plus its work. (d) **R-128:** the intraday timer's 16:45 ET slot is gone (in EDT it IS 20:45 UTC, the EOD writer's minute) and a held intraday pass no longer republishes the shared `vol-cone` snapshot or `data/vol_cone.json` — it has no live point, so the only honest write is the heartbeat, which still runs. Four unit hashes bumped in `installed-units.sha256`; owner docs updated for `ops-timers` and the client-ID table. Full gates: pytest `7518 passed, 1 skipped`; cloud failing-ID set identical to baseline (`34 failed, 913 passed`). |

| REL-052 (E) | DONE (R-124, R-125, R-135) | (this commit) | Display honesty, the fifth of five REL-052 tranches. RED: `23 failed` in the new `web/tests/panel-freshness-honesty.test.ts`. GREEN: `23 passed`. (a) **R-124:** all four new panels destructure `error` and render the new `PanelRefreshError` next to the header timestamp. `useSyncHook` KEEPS the previous `data` on a failed fetch, so the `error && !data` guard the older panels use leaves a background failure rendering last night's numbers as current — the affordance is for the `error && data` case the delta created. Brand tokens only (`var(--fault)`), no raw hex. (b) **R-125:** `/api/{trin,credit-spread,iei-hyg,vol-cone}` gate on `result.ok && result.fresh`, not `result.ok` alone. `/api/trin`'s 30-minute budget against a Mon-Fri 13:02-21:57 UTC writer meant every weekend served Friday's close as a current print. The collapse goes through the new `dbFirstRead.staleCollapse`, which carries the stale row's own `scan_time` and a `stale: true` marker onto the missing constant — gating on `fresh` and then serving a bare constant is exactly R-194's complaint about the two routes that already gated, so this tranche does not create four more instances of it. `staleCollapse` never mutates the frozen constant. (c) **R-135:** the cash-flows lozenge evaluates its throttled and errored branches BEFORE the no-prior-sync bail-out, with a `Never synced` prefix. A fresh environment (or a truncated `cash_flows`) that hits a 1025 on its first pull showed an empty table with no error, no retry hint and no tone for the full 7-day embargo — the failure state was visible only to an account that had already succeeded once. Full gates: pytest `7518 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7126 passed` (681 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| REL-052 (F) | DONE (R-126, R-131, R-136, R-137, R-138) | (this commit) | Silent-degradation bounds — the REL-052 remainder; the backlog row is now fully DONE. RED: `18 failed, 3 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_silent_degradation_bounds.py`. GREEN: `21 passed`. (a) **R-126:** `MIN_OBSERVATIONS == WINDOW_SESSIONS` and `_current` withholds both `state` and `ratio_pct_rank` on a partial window, publishing `window_complete` alongside the existing `window_sessions`. `extremes_window` is a trailing slice, so on a 30-session series the latest row IS the extreme nearly every day and the panel printed NEW 52W LOW/HIGH off a month of data — `fetch_ivrank` already refuses a partial-window rank for the same reason. Three existing `test_iei_hyg.py` cases pinned the old shape on a 62-session fixture; the inside-the-range classifier case was padded to a full window so it still tests what it was written for, and a partial-window control was added. `IeiHygState` gains `"unknown"` (label `BUILDING WINDOW`) and `ratio_pct_rank` becomes nullable. (b) **R-131:** a missing (or expiry-less) `flex_token_config.json` raises `HandlerSoftFailure` instead of returning `{"status": "skip"}`. `BaseHandler` treats every status but `error` as SUCCESS, so with the config deleted and gitignored the handler heartbeated a healthy `flex-token-check` daily while the Flex token marched to expiry unwatched. Returning `status: error` is forbidden by `test_base_handler_contract.py` (handlers must RAISE), hence the exception. The DUR-11 `service_health_events` prune only piggybacks on this handler because it is the daily 24/7 slot, so `execute()` now runs the sweep FIRST — a Flex soft failure must not take the retention sweep with it. (c) **R-136:** all three cancel and timeout branches go through the new bounded `_terminate_child`, and all three spawns take `start_new_session=True`. A child exiting between the `returncode is None` check and `proc.kill()` raised `ProcessLookupError` OUT of `except CancelledError`, replacing the cancellation; `await proc.wait()` was unbounded, so a SIGKILLed child in uninterruptible I/O held the cancelled task and its subprocess slot forever; and only the direct child was signalled, so a script's own children kept their IB client ids. The wait is shielded and `wait_for`-bounded at 5 s. (d) **R-137:** rsync carries `--chmod=F644` (the only mechanism that reaches a `user@host:` destination — `localMediaDest` returns null there, so the post-transfer chmod was a silent no-op on the DEFAULT route) and `--ignore-existing` is gone, so every pre-fix 0600 image can finally have its mode repaired instead of 403ing on media.radon.run permanently. `result.repairMode` records which mechanism ran. The local sweep gained `PUBLIC_MEDIA_EXTENSIONS`: it was chmod 0644-ing EVERY regular file in an operator-supplied directory. (e) **R-138:** the FRED overlay logs the series and the exception instead of swallowing it, so a revoked key / 429 / outage is no longer indistinguishable from "not released yet"; and an `actual` equal to `prev` is kept — UNRATE repeats month-over-month and ICSA ties weekly, and the observation-date staleness check is what actually separates released from not-yet-released. `test_apply_fred_actuals_skips_when_value_matches_prev` asserted the discard and was rewritten (renamed) onto the corrected behaviour. Full gates: pytest `7541 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7126 passed` (681 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean; cloud failing-ID set identical to baseline. |

| REL-068 (A) | DONE (R-165, R-167, R-168, R-169) | (this commit) | Money-path and relay guards, the first REL-068 tranche. RED: `13 failed` in the new `web/tests/relay-disconnect-honesty.test.ts` + `6 failed, 5 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_halt_and_throttle_bounds.py`. GREEN: `14 passed` + `11 passed`. (a) **R-165:** `modify_order` checks `trading_halt` itself. A modify RE-TRANSMITS the order, so it is a placement, and `trading_halt.py` states the contract as "every order-placing process on the host" — but the only kill switch on this path was the FastAPI route, which both the subprocess and the CLI go around. `cancel_order` is deliberately NOT gated and there is a test pinning that: cancelling reduces risk and cancel-all is the kill switch's own recovery path, so halting it would trap working orders. (b) **R-167:** `reconnectDelayMs(attempt)` — exponential from 5 s, +20% jitter, clamped at 120 s — replaces the flat `RECONNECT_MS`. `scheduleReconnect` re-arms from its own failure branch, so a Gateway that is down or sitting at 2FA was dialled 720 times an hour indefinitely, each attempt a fresh TCP connect + API handshake. The counter resets on the `connected` edge. (c) **R-168:** `isDisconnectedDuringDemand` + a `disconnected` verdict from `decideHealthWrite`, wired into the relay's stale-check loop with an `ib_disconnected` error row. While `ibConnected === false` during RTH the relay wrote NO `service_health` row at all — `decideStaleAction` returns `none`, `hasHealthyDataPlane` withholds the heartbeat and `isDataPlaneNulled` withholds `degraded` — so a relay whose socket was simply gone mid-session was indistinguishable, on every health surface, from one that is fine. Bounded by the same 45 s staleness threshold the tick ladder uses, so an ordinary reconnect never writes one. The 16 existing `staleDataMachine.test.js` cases assert the verdict object with `toEqual`; each gained the new field. (d) **R-169:** `is_blocked` fails CLOSED on an unparseable `blocked_until` and logs it, and `blocked_until` returns `CORRUPT_DEADLINE_SENTINEL` rather than `None` so "no lockout recorded" and "the record is corrupt" stop being the same answer. Unlike the 1025 lockout, which has the token-wide embargo standing the caller down behind it, nothing else bounds this path — a corrupt persisted string silently disarmed the 1018 rate-limit breaker. Full gates: pytest `7553 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7140 passed` (682 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| REL-068 (B) | DONE (R-163, R-164, R-166) | (this commit) | Display honesty, the second REL-068 tranche. RED: `10 failed, 2 passed` in the new `web/tests/display-honesty-delta.test.ts`. GREEN: `12 passed`. (a) **R-163:** `stale_nav` joins `GateReason` and `degradedReason` falls through to it, so a stale NAV suppresses annualized, both MWR figures and every risk statistic — not just the `twrCumReturn` ternary, which was `isStale`'s ONLY consumer. `gate()` was an identity function on a merely-stale payload, so a months-old snapshot published a full, confident performance card with one blank cell. `benchmarkReasonFor` deliberately lets the benchmark's own more specific cause (coverage, sample size, degeneracy) outrank `stale_nav` while a genuinely DEGRADED payload still wins outright — a complete-but-old block has a better answer than "stale". Three existing panel cases asserted published numbers off a fixed-date golden fixture read at the wall clock; each now reads on the fixture's own last session, which is the device that suite already documents for exactly this reason. (b) **R-164:** `HOLIDAY_TABLE_YEARS` + `isHolidayTableCovering` are exported and a contract test pins that the table covers the current year AND the next. `isHolidayIso` returns false for any uncovered year, so from 2028-01-01 `isUsTradingDay` silently degrades to weekday-only and the TODAY'S P&L gate stops suppressing the phantom holiday print. Runtime behaviour is deliberately unchanged — blanking an entire uncovered year is far worse than being wrong on ~9 days — so the exhaustion is converted into a CI failure a year ahead instead of a silent 2028. (c) **R-166:** `detectExecutedGroupStructure` takes the real per-leg quantity (summed across that leg's fills) and `detectStructure` names unequal 2-leg sizes `<type> Ratio Spread` / `<type> Backspread`. Threading the quantity alone was not enough — the shared classifier ignored the field entirely, so a filled 1x2 was labelled `Bull Call Spread`, a materially different risk profile, on every surface that names an executed group. Full gates: pytest `7553 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7152 passed` (683 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. (One vitest run under concurrent-pytest CPU contention showed a single failure that did not reproduce in two isolated re-runs.) |

| REL-068 (C) | DONE (R-189, R-190) | (this commit) | Data provenance, the third REL-068 tranche. RED: `8 failed, 4 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_data_provenance.py`. GREEN: `12 passed`. (a) **R-189:** `extract_index_value`'s bid/ask fallback accepts the DEGENERATE case only (`bid == ask`), and the snapshot ladder's readiness test moved to the new `index_has_data`. TRIN read a NYSE generated index's bid/ask as a spread in one function and as two distinct counts in the other: `_bid_ask` encodes the real convention (`adv, dec = bid, ask` for AD-NYSE, `up_vol, down_vol = bid, ask` for VOL-NYSE), so the midpoint of 1800 advancers and 900 decliners was a value of nothing. The AD-NYSE precedent the old docstring cited is one value published in both fields, which is exactly what survives. Tightening the value fallback alone would have made every counts-only ticker look unpriceable, hence the separate readiness predicate. `test_prefers_last_then_close_then_bid_ask_mid` asserted the midpoint and was rewritten (renamed) onto the degenerate case plus a control that two different counts yield no value. (b) **R-190:** `build_output` in both `fetch_credit_spread` and `fetch_iei_hyg` publishes `source_by_ticker`, and `fetch_iei_hyg.fetch_closes` returns the map alongside the collapsed string. `combine_source` set-joins the per-ticker dict, so an IB HYG with a Yahoo SPX and a Yahoo HYG with an IB SPX both stored `"ib+yahoo"` and which leg was the fallback was unrecoverable — for a RATIO indicator, that is the difference between a clean print and a cross-source artefact. The collapsed string is unchanged for compatibility. `test_payload_contract_from_fixture` pins the payload key set and took the new key; the CLI stub's `fetch_closes` return arity was updated. Full gate: pytest `7566 passed, 1 skipped`. |

| REL-068 (D) | DONE (R-176, R-177, R-178) | (this commit) | Unbounded I/O on hot paths, the fourth REL-068 tranche. RED: `13 failed, 2 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_unbounded_io_bounds.py`. GREEN: `15 passed`. (a) **R-176:** all three of the delta's new full-table reads are keyset-paginated — `fetch_trin._turso_daily` on `date`, `fetch_vol_cone._read_history_rows` on `(ticker, date)`, and `expiry_sweep._executed_orders_since` on `exec_id` (matching the keyset journal read already in that same file; the fill-time ordering the caller depends on is restored with a sort after the pages are collected). All three are append-only tables that grow forever, all three ran on the direct-to-cloud Hrana pipeline the §Turso Hrana I/O Bounding rule exists for, and in each case a paginated sibling was sitting next to the offender. (b) **R-177:** `_tail_findings` reads BACKWARDS in 64 KiB chunks until it holds more than `n` newlines, instead of slurping and JSON-parsing the entire WAL on every checkpoint. This is the exact pattern R-073 removed from `_repair_torn_findings_tail` in the same class, whose stated rationale — the file can be large and only the tail is needed — applies verbatim. The torn-final-line tolerance is preserved and pinned. (c) **R-178:** `prune_resolved` gives `data/incidents/` a 90-day retention on RESOLVED incidents and runs at the top of `record_cycle`, before the glob, so the cycle's own scan shrinks with it. `_open_incidents` re-globbed and JSON-parsed every incident ever written on all 288 cycles a day because status is only known after the parse. Fails safe in every ambiguous case: unparseable file, not-resolved, or resolved with no usable `resolved_at` are all left in place, and an open incident is never pruned however old. Full gate: pytest `7582 passed, 1 skipped`. |

| REL-068 (E) | DONE (R-172, R-173, R-174, R-175) | (this commit) | Resource accounting, the REL-068 remainder; the backlog row is now fully DONE. RED: `13 failed, 3 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_resource_accounting_bounds.py`. GREEN: `16 passed`. (a) **R-172:** `_read_unlocked` distinguishes "absent" from "present but unreadable", and `used()` reports the FULL daily limit spent on the latter. Collapsing a truncated state file (or an ENOSPC read) into `{}` silently reset the day's tally to zero, re-opening the entire 40,000-call budget to a scanner that had already spent it — the accounting went blind exactly when the disk filled. `usage_snapshot` carries `state_unreadable` so the operator surface can tell the two apart; the route's expected-payload helper took the new key. (b) **R-173:** `MAX_DISK_BYTES` joins `MAX_DISK_FILES` and the sweep reads `st_size` (it only ever read `st_mtime`). The payloads are whole UW greeks and `option-contracts` chains — hundreds of KB for a liquid name, ~4,800 candles for a daily OHLC pull — so a 3,000-FILE cap was a multi-GB ceiling on the VPS root fs. Both ceilings apply, oldest-first, whichever binds first. (c) **R-174:** the write-path prune is single-flight (`_prune_lock`) and rate-limited (`PRUNE_MIN_INTERVAL_SECONDS`). R-033 moved the sweep into `write_cache` so every writer maintains the cap, but `write_cache` runs inside `portfolio_performance`'s PARALLEL pool while `prune_cache`'s own docstring says "call ONCE after all parallel writes complete" — N threads each ran a full two-directory stat sweep and each computed an oldest-first list from a tree the others were mutating. REL-021's `test_write_path_prunes` pins R-033's contract and still does; it just clears the interval first. (d) **R-175:** `download` runs a fixed 4-worker pool over a shared index (order-preserving), caps images per post, and evicts the URL map at 2,000 entries. The fan-out was one concurrent request per `<img>` with `post.rawImages` taken verbatim from third-party article markup — the module's own comment says so — so the width, and the number of decoded buffers held at once, was attacker-influenced in a process that cycles every 120 s. Full gates: pytest `7599 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7152 passed` (683 files). |

| REL-069 (A) | DONE (R-179, R-180, R-181, R-182, R-186) | (this commit) | The auth perimeter, the first REL-069 tranche. RED: `13 failed, 3 passed` in the new `web/tests/auth-perimeter-delta.test.ts`. GREEN: `16 passed`. (a) **R-179:** `/api/ib/ws-ticket` takes `requireRouteAccess` and mints from `access.principal.token`. It made the Bearer OPTIONAL and had no route-local guard, so an anonymous caller reached `radonFetch("/ws-ticket")` with NO Authorization header — and the Next.js server is loopback to FastAPI, whose `/ws-ticket` trusts a loopback caller. The route was a deputy that minted a relay ticket for whoever asked. (b) **R-180:** the garch scan POST guards and rate-limits before any work. It SPAWNS `garch_convergence.py` and was classified as read-only market data, in a bucket whose own contract says a deliberate subprocess trigger belongs elsewhere — its `leap/scan` sibling has carried the identical guard since R-079. The read-only GET stays where it is. (c) **R-181:** `admin/demo-users` moves out of "routes with NO route-local guard" into a new `DEMO_ADMIN_GUARDED_ROUTES` bucket with a case that exercises `requireDemoAdmin` and names `DEMO_ADMIN_USER_IDS`. It HAS a guard, on a different allowlist, and filing it under "no guard" left that guard untested by either matrix. (d) **R-182:** the allowlist gate honours `admittedByDemoBlockade`. The admission was undone two lines later by `if (allowed.size > 0 && !allowlisted) return reject(403)`, so the documented escape hatch only existed on a deployment with no allowlist at all. (e) **R-186:** the probe-bearer path is rate-limited per client IP and served a reduced payload (`forProbe` nulls `last_error`/`error_summary`); the operator session still gets everything. Before 83de48e3 a bearer passed middleware and then 401'd in-route; afterwards it short-circuited `requireRouteAccess` entirely, so one shared STATIC token yielded every service's writer-supplied diagnostics at any rate. One existing ws-ticket smoke case asserted the raw-header forwarding and was rewritten onto the principal-minted shape (its source-level pins moved to the new suite, which has no `node:fs` mock to collide with). Full gates: pytest `7599 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7169 passed` (684 files); `tsc --noEmit` clean. |

| REL-069 (B) | DONE (R-193, R-194, R-195, R-196) | (this commit) | Serving-side honesty, the second REL-069 tranche. RED: `13 failed, 4 passed` in the new `web/tests/serving-honesty-delta.test.ts`. GREEN: `17 passed`. (a) **R-193:** `/api/{credit-spread,iei-hyg,trin,vol-cone}` pass `isDegraded: isMissingPayload` to `dbFirstRead`, the guard vixcor and ivrank already carried. `pickFresherSource` compares on timestamp alone without it, so a writer that ran, produced nothing and still stamped a timestamped row outranked an older row holding a real series. (d) **R-196:** `FLAGGABLE_REGIME_TABS` names the four tabs `buildRailStatuses` can actually emit, `elevatedCount` counts over it, and the rail foot reads `4 MONITORED · N ELEVATED`. The summary was denominated over all 22 `REGIME_TABS` while only `cri`/`cor`/`vcg`/`gex` can ever be flagged — the five indicators this delta added are registered tabs with no rail reading — so "0 of 22 elevated" read as broad calm when 18 were never asked. | REL-069 (C) | DONE (R-183, R-184, R-185, R-188) | (this commit) | The control plane, the third REL-069 tranche. RED: `12 failed, 4 passed` in the new `cloud/tests/test_control_plane_bounds.py`. GREEN: `16 passed`. (a) **R-183:** all THREE `sync_scheduled_units || return 1` call sites warn instead of failing the deploy. Every one of them runs after the release is verified, the green marker written and the transition committed — a failed unit publish cannot un-deploy any of that — and the helper's exit 76 (`local HEAD != git ls-remote origin main`) is exactly what a mid-deploy push to main produces. A benign race was red-flagging a green release and stranding the transition journal; the next deploy republishes the units. The finding cited one site; the other two had the identical shape. (b) **R-184:** the `enable --now` loop tries every new timer, names each failure (`install-units: enable failed <unit>`), and returns 1 at the END. `|| return $?` on the first failure silently skipped every LATER new timer, and neither the deploy nor the drift audit noticed the ones never attempted. (c) **R-185:** both weekend wrappers disarm the ERR trap across the agent loop and re-arm it after. `timeout claude` returning 124 (the cap) or any non-zero agent exit is an EXPECTED outcome the loop handles, but the trap was armed over it, so every failed or timed-out run posted a false "CRASHED — wrapper died" dead-man comment AND then its real status — which is worse than no dead-man, because the operator learns to ignore it. `reliability_weekend.sh` has the identical bug (its `timeout` call sits inside `run_round()`, defined above the loop, so the positional shape differs but the exposure does not) and is fixed in the same commit. A genuine wrapper death after the agent finishes still pages. (d) **R-188:** `EXPECTED_AUTO_SYNC_UNITS` pins the auto-sync allowlist's exact membership, with a pointer left next to the per-entry shape checks in `test_sync_scheduled_units.py`. Those checks validated each ENTRY and nothing bounded the LIST: adding a unit grants CI the right to publish it into `/etc/systemd/system` with no root bootstrap, which is a decision, not a detail. A companion case pins that `install-units` is bounded by the manifest digest rather than an allowlist, so nobody resolves the asymmetry by widening `install-units` instead. Full gates: pytest `7599 passed, 1 skipped`; `cloud/tests` `34 failed, 929 passed` — the failing-ID set is identical to the stashed baseline (`34 failed, 913 passed`), the darwin `sha256sum` set, with the +16 delta being this tranche's own new cases. |

| REL-069 (D) | DONE (R-170, R-171, R-187) | (this commit) | Cadence and growth bounds — the REL-069 remainder; the backlog row, and the whole 2026-08-22 backlog, is now DONE. RED: `17 failed, 3 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_cadence_and_growth_bounds.py`. GREEN: `20 passed`. (a) **R-170:** a 502/503 that survives the retries returns the new `SHED_EXIT=75` and the wrapper exits 0 with a named capacity message; the timer moves to minute :05. General-lane capacity is `MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBPROCESSES(4) - RESERVED_ORDER_SLOTS(1) = 3`, and `radon-garch` (14:00 UTC) and `radon-leap` (10:00 ET = 14:00 UTC in EDT) each hold a slot for up to 3600 s, so two signals scans deterministically contended for the last one — `RETRY_LIMIT(2) x RETRY_DELAY(8)` = 16 s of waiting against a 3600 s hold, then a correct refusal of the direct fallback, exit 1, and a P1 page every hour for something no retry can fix. The wrapper runs hourly, so the next slot picks the scan up, and R-187's tightened windows page if it never does. The direct-fallback refusal is UNCHANGED and still pinned: a shed must never duplicate an in-flight scan. `test_persistent_502_still_fails_without_direct_fallback` asserted the paging behaviour and was rewritten (renamed) onto the shed, keeping its fallback assertions verbatim. (b) **R-171:** `pruneMediaTree` gives the media tree a 90-day retention plus a 5,000-file cap, runs from the push, and the full-tree permission sweep is rate-limited to once an hour. There was no unlink, prune or tmpfiles.d for that tree ANYWHERE, so every image ever scraped was retained forever as a re-encoded PNG at compressionLevel 9 up to 16 MP, while `ensurePublicMediaPermissions` `readdir`+`stat`ed all of it every 2 minutes at a cost growing linearly in a directory that only grows. The disk watchdog is alert-only and its own message says "prune caches/logs before it wedges the box" — for this tree there was nothing to prune with. The pruner reuses R-137's extension allowlist. (c) **R-187:** `open` drops from 4d to 3h on both `theta-harvester` and `strength-confirmation`, in the watchdog AND `serviceHealthWindows.ts`, with `closed`/`extended` still at 4d. 4d was 96x an HOURLY cadence: a timer dead on Monday morning went unreported until Thursday while the Top-candidates panel served a stale row. R-068 chose 4d because a Friday-afternoon row must not page at Monday 09:31 — that belongs to the open-bell grace, so the web side adds both to `RTH_ONLY_SERVICES` and the Python side gets a new `OPEN_BELL_GRACE_SERVICES` frozenset that `check.py` honours alongside `BUCKETS["intraday"]`. The bucket was the wrong key: it answers how often the WATCHDOG polls, not whether the writer is RTH-only, and these two are hourly RTH writers on the daily check cadence. The existing Monday-morning no-false-page case passes unchanged; a new case pins that 4.5 h of mid-session silence now trips. Full gates: pytest `7620 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7189 passed`; `cloud/tests` failing-ID set identical to baseline (`34 failed`). |

**Gate unblock (not a REL task):** `54ba227c` — `web/tests/previous-close-yahoo-daily-array.test.ts` still spaced its fixture by CALENDAR days, so on any weekend run index 3 ("yesterday's close") landed on a Saturday, the route correctly skipped it, and the fixture failed. Same class as `e585a9f7`. The builder now walks trading days via `isUsTradingDay` and stamps each bar at the 16:00 ET close. This was the only red in an otherwise green vitest run and blocked the full-gate contract for every weekend loop.

**Exit evidence:** the nine permanent fault-injection drill suites all green — `test_position_reconcile_spine`, `test_exit_orders_ack`, `test_exit_orders_guard_durability`, `test_trading_halt`, `test_order_limits`, `test_fill_monitor_degraded_session`, `test_daemon_bounded`, `test_snapshot_unavailable` (`77 passed` together) plus `order-idempotency-durability` (`12 passed`). Four new drills join the permanent set: REL-023 (kill switch admits through a saturated subprocess pool), REL-024 (dual-id-convention re-insert), REL-026 (replace refuses with zero cancels), REL-030 (stripped-env stand-down + daily cap).

**Runner-environment note:** this clone's python3.13 had no `pytest-asyncio`, which failed 98 async tests before any work started (CI installs it in `ci.yml`). Installed; the pre-change baseline is `6365 passed`. Separately, 10 `cloud/tests` cases fail on darwin only — they assert on a `sha256sum` binary that does not exist on macOS (`shutil.which("sha256sum")` is `None`); they pass in Linux CI and are unaffected by this run's changes.

| REL-047 | DONE | (this commit) | R-100, R-101, R-102, R-104, R-108, R-109 (P1) + R-129, R-130 (P2) — the Flex embargo becomes one durable, monotonic, shared state. RED: `12 errors` in the new `scripts/tests/test_flex_embargo_durability.py` (verified by stashing all four source files and re-running, so every case is red against the shipped code, not just against a missing import). (b) **R-104 shared:** `is_due` consults `flex_embargo.active_until()` before its private `_backoff_state`, and `seed_state_after_corrupt_load` prefers the shared sidecar over the `service_health` fallback — a lockout armed by perf-twr or the blotter no longer leaves the daily window open to spawn a subprocess that dies on the embargo and (per R-100) extends it. (c) **R-129 atomic:** the sidecar goes through `utils.atomic_io.atomic_save`, so a reader in the microseconds after a truncate can no longer see empty JSON and SendRequest into a live lockout. (d) **R-130 durable + honest:** `_rehydrate_from_health` reads the deadline back out of the `service_health` dual-write the module header always claimed existed, so deleting the gitignored sidecar no longer silently drops a live 7-day lockout; and a lapse now writes an `ok` row via `_heartbeat_ok`, so the `flex-web-service` banner stops sitting red for ~24 h quoting a `next_attempt_at` in the past. (e) **R-108 seeding:** `load_state` seeds handlers when the state file is MISSING (it used to return before the hook, which only fired on a checksum failure — a fresh host or restored VM forgot a live 24 h-168 h embargo), and `set_state` is per-handler try/except so one malformed value no longer aborts the restore loop, re-seeds correctly-restored handlers, and writes a spurious `.corrupt-<ts>` backup of a VALID file. (f) **R-109 generation guard:** `retire_current_run()` is called from the daemon's deadline path BEFORE `_record_deadline_kill`, and `execute()` stamps a generation at entry — a late worker thread's `_mark_success` is refused, so it can no longer clear `blocked_until` and write an `ok` row over the embargo the deadline path just recorded. (g) **R-101/R-102:** `POST /performance` now runs the same embargo + cooldown gate as `/performance/background` (its callers include the cold-start GET path, so an empty `performance_snapshots` made every GET block on a full builder run — the request storm that earned the 1025); the cooldown moved from a process-local `time.monotonic()` float to an atomic sidecar, so a deploy, watchdog restart or OOM can no longer reset the 20-minute floor to zero; and refusals are real status codes (503 lockout / 429 cooldown) with a log line instead of a 202 to a caller that swallows the body. Two existing suites pinned the old contract and were rewritten onto the new one with the reason inline (`test_performance_background_cooldown.py`'s monotonic fixture and dict-shaped refusals; `test_performance_lock.py` gained an autouse fixture isolating the sidecar from the repo's real `data/` — it patches BOTH `api.server` and `scripts.api.server`, which are two module objects for one file in this tree). Full gates: pytest `7347 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7061 passed` (676 files); cloud failing-ID set identical to baseline. |

An unreadable age counts as stranded: "we could not read its mtime" is not a reason to keep excusing kills. (b) **R-158:** `nextjs-db-read` joins the `continuous` bucket. The `error` bucket is auto-derived from every scheduled service, so membership there is not evidence of staleness checking — `_check_error` fires solely on `state == "error"`, and a stopped or disabled `radon-nextjs-db-watchdog.timer` left the last `ok` row un-age-checked forever (a stopped timer is `inactive`, not `failed`, so `units.py` misses it too). A new contract asserts NO scheduled service is staleness-unchecked, with `watchdog-alerts` the one documented exemption (age-checking the meta-row this service writes about itself is a recursive alerting loop). (c) **R-159:** `perf-twr` gets a window in both catalogs (26 h open / 4 d closed, matching its Tue..Sat 07:30 ET timer and the Sat→Tue gap) and the builder writes its own row through the new `_record_perf_twr_health` / `_perf_twr_state`. It wrote NO row and always exited 0 — `--check` is the only non-zero path and the unit's `ExecStart` does not pass it — so on a Flex 1025 lockout the builder returned `degraded`, systemd recorded success, `check.py` treated "no row" as dormant, and `/performance` served stale returns with no alert. `stale` maps to an ERROR row deliberately: `--check` tolerates it because a stale-but-valid payload is still servable, but a builder that cannot advance the curve is exactly what this row exists to surface. One existing case (`test_signal_kill_during_inflight_deploy_downgrades_to_p3`) passed a bare `in_flight: True` and now supplies a fresh journal age, with a new sibling pinning that a STRANDED journal keeps the kill at P1. Full gates: pytest `7432 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7088 passed` (679 files); cloud failing-ID set identical to baseline. |

**Gate unblock (not a REL task):** `test_run_portfolio_refresh_retry.py::test_server_up_after_first_refusal_succeeds` bound its late HTTP server after a fixed `time.sleep(0.5)` and raced bash startup in BOTH directions — bind first and there is no "retry" line to assert, bind after the ladder drains and the exit code is non-zero. Measured 3 failures in 5 runs on a CLEAN tree (verified by stashing every change), so it would have broken the three-consecutive-green contract for any weekend loop. The bind is now driven by the script's own "retry" line read off a `Popen` pipe, inside a 3 s retry delay: `7 passed` x5 consecutively.

| REL-070 (B) | DONE (R-201, R-202); R-264 split — see below | (this commit) | CI gate integrity: nothing untested may reach the production VPS. (b) **R-202:** `scripts/lib/` joins `WEB_PREFIXES`. `vitest.config.ts:18` includes `scripts/lib/**/*.test.js` (eleven suites covering the WS relay's stale-tick ladder, reconnect gate and backpressure) and pytest collects none of them, but `path_filter.classify` routed the path to the python gate alone, so `web-tests` was **skipped** and `deploy` accepts `skipped`. Verified `classify(["scripts/lib/staleDataMachine.js"])` returned `(True, False)` before and `(True, True)` after; an ordinary `scripts/*.py` change still skips vitest. **Pinned test rewritten, deliberately:** `test_ci_deploy_concurrency.py::test_stage_release_overlaps_gating_jobs_and_is_cancelable` asserted `"needs" not in stage` and `continue-on-error in ("true", True)` — it pinned the hole itself. Both assertions are inverted; the cancelability and separate-concurrency-group half, which is what keeps a superseded prestage from colliding with the real deploy, is unchanged. Full gates: pytest `8009 passed, 1 skipped` (with REL-071); vitest `7498 passed` (723 files); cloud failing-ID set byte-identical to a stashed clean-tree baseline (34 darwin-only, `setup-vps.sh:713 owner_args[@]: unbound variable` — bash 3.2 treats an empty array as unbound under `set -u`; Linux CI runs bash 5). |
| REL-070 (c) | R-264 — NOT A DEFECT (a) / DECLINED WITH REASON (b) | (this commit) | Both halves were tested rather than taken on the finding's word, and neither survived. **(b) the "dead" case pattern is not dead.** The finding reasons that `cloud/config/*\| \` + newline + six-space indent leaves `"      requirements*"` as the effective glob. Bash tokenizes `case` alternatives as words, so the indentation is not part of the pattern: `case "$1" in web/*\| \<newline>      requirements*) echo MATCH;; esac` with `$1=requirements.txt` prints `MATCH`. The new `cloud/tests/test_payload_case_patterns.py` extracts the runtime arm's glob **from `deploy.sh` itself** and evaluates real paths against it — `requirements.txt`, `requirements-dev.txt`, `pyproject.toml`, `bun.lock`, `package.json` and `*.service` all match, `docs/*.md` does not (`3 passed` against unmodified HEAD). Kept as a standing guard so an edit that genuinely breaks the arm fails. **(a) `site/` must stay on the tests-only `continue`.** The finding is right that `site/lib` is executed by the root vitest config, but `payload_paths_changed` decides whether to **restart the fleet**, not whether to run tests — the test gate already covers `site/` through `path_filter`'s `WEB_PREFIXES`. No VPS unit serves `site/`: `radon-nextjs.service:16` is `WorkingDirectory=/home/radon/radon/web`, no `cloud/services/*` references the tree, and `site/vercel.json` + `site/next.config.ts` are the deploy target. Moving it off the `continue` would stop and restart every live trading service for a marketing-site copy change. Declined; the reasoning is pinned by the arm test above. |

(a) **R-198:** both completeness guards in `_replay_contract` are quantity-only, but `_journal_entry` drops a row for non-quantity reasons, and a dropped row removes its quantity **and its cost together**, so the two errors cancel exactly and no guard fires. The taint is deliberately narrow — rows with no full contract key (BAG envelopes, stock) and rehydrated `CLOSED` round trips carry no net quantity and are excluded by design (T-124), so they do not taint; a broken row on one contract does not taint a sibling contract (asserted). (b) **R-203:** the unpaginated `SELECT ... WHERE ticker IN (...)` with no LIMIT and no date bound now goes through `journal_basis._fetch_journal_rows_for_tickers`, the same 200-row keyset pager the sibling module was converted to, and `overlay_journal_realized_pnl` defaults to a bounded Hrana reader instead of `db.client.get_db()` — the sync `libsql_experimental` connection whose own docstring states it exposes no execute timeout and holds the GIL while blocked. This path runs inside `service_cycle("orders-sync")` before the per-execId upserts and at the head of `_mirror_executed_orders`, over a monotonically growing table. Asserted: 450 rows are read in exactly 3 statements of `LIMIT 200`, and the overlay fails the test if it reaches `get_db()`. Three pinned tests updated for the transport move (`test_ib_orders_dual_write.py`, `test_evening_execution_sweep.py`, `TestOverlayWithDb`) — their fakes now stamp `trade_id` and honour cursor + LIMIT; every assertion they made is preserved. | REL-072 (B) | DONE (R-199) | (this commit) | GARCH: a UW quota or auth failure no longer synthesizes the most bullish IV rank. RED: `5 failed, 8 passed` in `scripts/tests/test_garch_convergence_scanner.py`. GREEN: `13 passed`. `_fetch_uw_iv` caught `except UWAPIError: pass` and returned a literal `(0.0, 0.0)`; `UWRateLimitError` and `UWAuthError` both subclass it, so a 429 or a rejected token set `iv_rank = 0.0` with `error` left `None`. Zero is not a neutral placeholder here — it is the **most** bullish value every downstream threshold tests for (gate 4 wants `< 50`, the STRONG tier wants `< 30`), and a fabricated `current_iv = 0.0` maximised the HV-IV gap on the same path, so a quota-exhausted run manufactured STRONG long-vol ideas that were indistinguishable from a healthy scan. `_fetch_uw_iv` now returns `(None, None)` and warns on stderr in the module's own style; `TickerVol.iv_rank`/`current_iv` are `Optional`; `_analyze_ticker` stamps `tv.error = "UW IV rank unavailable"` while KEEPING the HV series, which came from a different source. `analyze_pair` gates on it beside the `has_leaps` gates, with a comment recording why `has_leaps` was never cover: it comes from a different UW endpoint and says nothing about whether the IV-rank call succeeded. `to_json` carries `error`, and `web/lib/types.ts:GarchTickerVol` gains the matching optional field (`lagger_iv_rank: number \| null` and a null-case test were already there). Two rendering sites that would have raised on a null (`{pa.lagger_iv_rank:.0f}%` at the gate table and the pair summary, neither behind the `tv.error` guard the ticker table has) go through a new `_iv_rank_cell`. `PairAnalysis.lagger_iv_rank` also moved to `Optional`: it kept its `0.0` dataclass default on a refused pair, which put a plausible `0%` into the payload and the report for a pair that was never analyzed. Full gates: pytest `8016 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7498 passed` (723 files). |
| REL-073 (B) | DONE (R-200, R-228, R-230) | (this commit) | A dead indicator feed no longer serves a fabricated calm reading at HTTP 200. RED: `5 failed` (regime route), `7 failed` (CRI display resolution), `4 failed` (vcg route), `no tests` (retry schedule module absent). GREEN: `5 + 7 + 4 + 8 passed`. (a) **R-200:** both `/api/regime` source readers swallow every failure into `null`, and `GET` substituted the literal `EMPTY_CRI` — `score: 0`, `level: "LOW"`, four zeroed components, `crash_trigger.triggered: false`, `cta.exposure_pct: 200` — then overwrote `market_open` with the live session so the payload looked current and returned 200 with `cacheState: "HIT"`. None of those defaults is neutral: they are the CALMEST reading the panel can draw, and every one is also a legal real value, so "CRI is 0" and "CRI is unknown" were byte-identical to the client. The route now returns the envelope with `missing: true`, `cri`/`cta`/`crash_trigger` nulled, `cacheState: "MISS"`, `max-age: 0` and an `X-Sync-Warning` header (which previously existed only on POST). (b) **panel half of R-200:** `RegimePanel` branched on `data?.cri ?` — always truthy because the route always shipped the object — and carried its OWN second `{score: 0, level: "LOW"}` default on the same line, so fixing the route alone would still have painted `0 /100` in `levelColor("LOW")` with four `ComponentBar`s coloured `var(--positive)` by the `score < 8` test. The decision moved into a pure `resolveCriDisplay` beside the existing `resolveCrashTriggerState`, and the panel returns the `SectionEmptyState` shell it already had for no-data. Deliberately preserved: a genuine `0`/`LOW` reading still renders (asserted), and a live VIX/VVIX/SPY-derived `liveCri` still wins over a dead cache, so the guard does not blank a panel that has real numbers. `railStatuses` was also reading `cri.score` unconditionally and now reports no CRI status rather than a zero one. (c) **R-228:** the identical shape on `/api/vcg` — `normalizeVcgPayload(cached ?? {})` fabricated `regime: "DIVERGENCE"`, `interpretation: "NORMAL"`, `ro: 0`/`edr: 0` (which SUPPRESS the RISK-OFF and EDR pills), so a trader read an affirmative "no risk-off signal" out of a dead feed, with the Z-score `---` as the only tell. Same treatment: `missing: true`, `signal: null`, and `VcgPanel` gains the degraded branch its `if (!data) return null` guard could never reach. (d) **R-230:** `needsCurrentEtSessionRetry` returns `true` unconditionally on `!scan_time`, and the fabricated payload carried exactly that, so the hook dropped from 60 s to a 5 s GET loop that re-armed after every response — the response being retried was precisely the one that could never satisfy the predicate. 12 req/min per tab against a route limited to 20/min, and every stale GET also kicked `triggerBackgroundScan()`, so the busier the loop the less likely the recovery scan got through. New `resolveRetryDelayMs` gives exponential backoff with a 60 s ceiling and a 6-attempt cap; `useSyncHook`'s two identical arming sites (the request path and the initial-read path — the finding cited one) both route through a single `armRetry` whose streak counter resets on a payload that no longer needs retrying. Full gates: vitest `7522 passed` (727 files), `tsc --noEmit` clean; pytest `8016 passed, 1 skipped`. |

(a) **R-208:** `comboQuotePriceData` stamped `new Date().toISOString()` on every call, and `hasLiveQuote` rejects only on a stale timestamp — so the `CLOSE/HIGH/LOW` downgrade was structurally unreachable for a combo, on all five surfaces a trader prices one from. New `oldestQuoteTimestamp` threads the worst leg's stamp through `resolveNaturalSpreadQuote`, `computeNetOptionQuote`, `useTargetQuote` and the two signed-net memos into all five call sites; an unknown leg yields `null`, which the wrapper turns into a not-live quote rather than "now". **A first attempt inferred `lastIsCalculated` from `last == null` and was wrong** — every current caller passes the net mid, so that flipped four `MARK` labels to `LAST` and broke their tests. It is now an explicit caller-supplied field defaulting to `true`, which is what the finding actually asks for: the wrapper should not assert "calculated" about a value it knows nothing about. (b) **R-271:** `TickerDetailContent` synthesized a `PriceData` whose `last` is the portfolio-sync mark, potentially hours old, and stamped it with the render clock; `BookTab` ages exactly that object. Asserted with fake timers: a fresh panel downgrades to `CLOSE` after six minutes with identical props and no other re-render. (d) **R-254:** the futures ticket's only quote block is the cash index, and `ListedContractOrderForm`'s own prop doc says `priceData` is "the SELECTED future / index option, never the cash underlying". Rather than drop the index (it is useful context), a new `quoteProvenance` line renders OUTSIDE the panel — the `Index` suffix was inside the panel the numbers are in, which is not a disclosure the operator reads on the way to the limit-price input. (e) **R-255:** the modify modal's nine-field panel was fed `applyRestingLimitToQuote(...)`, so a SELL resting at 5.00 against a true 7.00/8.00 printed `ASK 5.00`, `MID 6.00`, `SPREAD 2.00 / 33%` — a spread measured against the operator's own order, at full market weight. A BAG is also captioned `<symbol> net` instead of the bare underlying ticker. **Pinned test rewritten:** `order-ticket-spread-notional.test.ts` asserted the doctored `$0.60 / 14.29%` in the panel — it pinned the defect. (b) **R-211:** `remaining_lock_secs` called `check_2fa_push_lock` positionally and dropped `guard_timeout_secs`, and `consume_2fa_push_lock` took a bare `LOCK_EX`, so `_guard` used the blocking branch — while the guard is held across `_write_lock_file`'s two `os.fsync` calls. (b) **R-220:** the final `handle` block serves `POST /api/orders/place`, `/cancel` and `/modify` with a 15 s retry loop and no `retry_match`, so order non-duplication rested on an unpinned third-party default while this path has no idempotency key at all (R-009, R-022) and the operator's only evidence would be two fills. `retry_match { method GET HEAD }` states it. Both fault-injection cases the finding says were uncovered in either direction now exist: a `_NeverAnswers` upstream must produce a 5xx within a bound, and a POST severed mid-flight (what `boundedShutdown`'s `exit(143)` does to a draining connection) must reach the upstream exactly once. The two orphan tests were rewritten to model a genuinely transient failure (fails once, answers on retry) rather than a permanently dead port. (b) **R-227:** `_upsert_holder_rows` writes N chunks under a single `commit()`, so a failure on chunk k left chunks 0..k-1 applied and k..N absent — and since the upsert is not preceded by a delete, that truncated set mixed silently with the previous quarter's rows while the snapshot (carrying the full `holders` array and `holder_count`) asserted completeness and the health row said `ok`. The write now rolls back as a unit, and the snapshot carries `holders_persisted` so a consumer can see when the depth rows are behind the summary. The chunk-parameter builder was extracted so the guarded loop stays readable. (c) **R-260:** `garch_convergence.py` and `leap_scanner_uw.py` wrote their canonical caches with a plain `write_text` while the repo ships `utils.atomic_io.atomic_save` and `bpi_scan.py` uses it for the same job. `data/garch_convergence.json` has no Turso table behind it, so a SIGTERM or a full disk mid-write truncated the only copy and `/api/garch-convergence` served a `JSONDecodeError` with nothing to fall back to. Both now use `atomic_save`; the assertion strips comment lines before checking for `write_text`, so the explanatory comment naming the old call cannot satisfy or break it. **Docs contract:** `scripts/tests/test_docs_contract.py` required an owner-doc update for the two mapped Equibles paths — `docs/equibles-api.md` gains the fatal-error and holder-atomicity contracts in the same commit. | REL-086 (B) | DONE (R-229) | (this commit) | A missing risk-free rate no longer reads as 0%. RED: `4 failed, 1 passed` in the new `web/tests/risk-free-rate-unavailable.test.tsx`. The route answers any failure with `{rate: 0, source: "fallback", stale: true}` at HTTP 200; the hook correctly refused to cache that but then left `cachedRate === null` with only a `useEffect(..., [])` per consumer mount, and `loadRiskFreeRate` short-circuits on `inFlight` — no timer, no retry, no backoff, no error state. On a long-lived `WorkspaceShell` session one transient FRED miss at page load pinned `r = 0` for the rest of the session, and `PositionTable` feeds that straight into `computePositionImpliedValue` for the Implied columns the operator compares against Last Price to judge whether a spread is mispriced. New `useRiskFreeRateState()` returns `{rate: number \| null, resolved}`; `useRiskFreeRate()` is kept as `state.rate ?? 0` so the five other consumers are untouched. An exponential retry ladder (60 s base, 15 min ceiling) arms on any non-observation and clears on a real one — asserted three ways: a single failure recovers on the timer with nothing else mounting, a persistent failure backs off rather than hammering, and a success schedules nothing. `PositionTable` moves to the nullable state and every implied call site — the two sort keys, the two row memos and the leg-row branch — is guarded, so the columns render "—" rather than a Black-Scholes number priced off an unknown rate. **Two pinned suites updated, deliberately:** `position-table-implied.test.tsx` and `position-table-short-leg-sign.test.tsx` compute their expected values with `bsPut(..., 0, ...)` — they were silently relying on the unresolved-means-zero default. A new `seedRiskFreeRateForTests(0)` seam installs a RESOLVED 0%, which is a legal reading, so their arithmetic is unchanged and the assumption is now explicit; the unresolved case is asserted separately. NOT done and left with the finding: the four other consumers (`ModifyOrderModal`, `WorkspaceSections`, `OptionsChainTab`, `OrderTab`) still take the `?? 0` collapse. The acceptance criteria name `PositionTable`, and each of the others needs its own render decision; the nullable state is now available to them. | REL-087 (B) | DONE (R-231, R-233, R-234, R-235); R-232 PARTIAL, remedy corrected | (this commit) | **⚠ Control plane: this commit edits `cloud/services/*/runtime-container.conf.example` and `cloud/scripts/*`, so the root `bootstrap-control-plane.sh` install-copy is required before merge.** RED: `11 failed, 1 passed, 5 skipped` in the new `cloud/tests/test_app_plane_cutover_safety.py`. The same branch set `UNITS_RESTARTED=0`, which forced `remaining=0` in `check_units_stable`; that special case is gone, because the path that mutates the live tree under running services is precisely the one where an unexplained unit failure has no restart to correlate with. (b) **R-232 — the finding's remedy does not work; a different one does.** Pointing `--cgroup-parent` at the unit is not available: Docker's systemd cgroup driver accepts a slice, not a unit path, and `cloud/tests/test_app_runtime.py` already asserts `--cgroup-parent=system.slice` with that reason inline. A first attempt at the finding's fix broke all five app-runtime cases. So the container will always sit in `system.slice/docker-<id>.scope` and `KillMode=control-group` will always reach only the `docker run` client — the reachable half is reaping the container explicitly, which is what actually produced the `Conflict` → `start-limit-hit` outcome: `docker rm -f "$unit"` on the way in, and a new `stop` verb called from `ExecStopPost=` in each drop-in on the way out. The cgroup-membership half is recorded as not-fixable-as-written. (c) **R-233:** `app-images.yml` passed no `--build-arg`, so the three `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` ARGs were inlined into the pushed client bundle as empty strings — and `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is compile-time inlined, so `radon-app-runtime`'s `--env-file` cannot repair it: no Clerk publishable key and no API base URL for every browser session the moment the drop-ins are installed. The build now passes all three and FAILS the job if any is empty, rather than shipping a silently broken bundle. (d) **R-234:** `image_tag()` pinned the exact deploy SHA with no fallback and no existence check, while the image workflow sets `cancel-in-progress` and `ci.yml`'s deploy deliberately does not wait on it — so a second push inside the build budget left SHA1's tags unpushed and all five app units hit `manifest unknown` at once. `resolve_image` now preflights with `docker manifest inspect` and falls back to `RADON_APP_IMAGE_FALLBACK_TAG` (default `latest`), logging the substitution; if neither exists it exits 69 rather than handing docker a tag it will reject. (e) **R-235:** every drop-in example resets `ExecStart=` but not `ExecStartPre=`, so the base unit's migrate step still ran — as root on the host, against production Turso — and would 203/EXEC the moment the host `.venv` is retired. All five per-unit examples now reset it. Note the finding overstates the blast radius slightly: only `radon-api.service` has an `ExecStartPre` to inherit today, so the other four resets are pre-emptive; the test skips where the base unit has nothing to reset. **Two pinned tests rewritten:** `test_usage_is_pull_or_run_unit` (the `stop` verb is new) and `test_check_units_stable_skips_sleep_when_units_were_not_restarted`, whose assertion is inverted with the reasoning inline — everything else it checked is unchanged. **Self-inflicted trap avoided twice:** a slice keyed on `stop_services_for_transition` ended inside the branch's own comment naming that function, and the drop-in patch script aborted on the fleet-wide example that declares no `ExecStart`; both are handled explicitly. Full gates: pytest `8092 passed, 1 skipped`; `cloud/tests` byte-identical to baseline; vitest unchanged at `7564 passed`. |
| REL-088 (B) | DONE (R-236, R-268) + NF-8 closed | (this commit) | Watchdog catalog parity, and the standing sweep turned into a test. (a) **R-236:** `breadth-scan` is a scheduled writer with zero staleness alarm on either side. `radon-breadth.timer` fires every five minutes across ET trading hours into `run_breadth_scan.sh` → `breadth_scan.py`'s `mirror_scan_snapshot("breadth-scan", ...)`, but `SCHEDULED_SERVICES` had no key for it, so it appeared in no `BUCKETS` list and `check.py` — which iterates `BUCKETS` and nothing else — never evaluated it; and the web catalog said `category: "on-demand"`, which coerces a past-window row into the informational `dormant` state instead of the degraded `stale` one. Disable the timer and breadth data froze silently on both surfaces. `units.py` is not a backstop: it fires only on `ActiveState=failed`, which a soft or no-op exit does not produce. Registered in both catalogs at `15 min` open / `3 d` closed, matching its Mon-Fri-only sibling `vcg-scan`, and added to the `intraday` bucket. `requires_ib: true` is verified from source the way that contract test demands (`breadth_scan.py:59` imports `DEFAULT_HOST`, `:118` calls `ib.connect`), and the pinned `test_requires_ib_true_set_matches_verified_writers` set was extended with that citation inline. (b) **NF-8 closed:** the standing sweep compared only the DELTA's own jobs against the catalogs, which is exactly why a timer whose job has written this heartbeat since before the audit anchor was never checked. It is now a test that ENUMERATES every service name reachable from a `cloud/services/*.timer` and asserts membership in both catalogs — the sweep is code, not a procedure. Two false positives had to be designed out first: state literals (`"ok"`, `"error"`) picked up from helper wrappers, and `informed-flow` attributed to `radon-equibles-13f` because the enumeration followed every module a script merely mentioned. A `.sh` ExecStart is followed one level (it is a wrapper); a `.py` ExecStart is the job and is not followed. An `EXEMPT` map exists for deliberate non-checks, and is empty. (c) **R-268:** the `flap` bucket cannot fire for any DUR-02-braked unit — `auto-restart` is occupied only for `RestartSec` per restart while the brake parks the unit `failed` in `RestartSec × StartLimitBurst` (~25 s for `radon-api`), so two five-minute samples cannot both land inside a five-second window. This is not an outage gap: `Result=start-limit-hit` does page P1. The bucket is KEPT rather than retired — an unbraked `radon-*` unit looping with a long `RestartSec` is a shape only `flap` catches — but the module docstring now states the limitation, so it no longer reads as two independent P1 signals when it has one. A test derives the parking time from each unit file and asserts it against the cycle interval, so the claim cannot rot. Full gates: pytest `8100 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest `7564 passed` (735 files). |
| REL-089 (B) | DONE (R-237, R-238, R-239, R-240, R-266, R-267) | (this commit) | The weekend loops' own dead-man contract. RED: `12 failed, 2 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_weekend_loop_deadman.py`. GREEN: `17 passed`, `52 passed` on the self-rewrite suite, `32 passed` across all weekend suites. (a) **R-237:** `ground_truth` was called bare inside `run_phase` with `trap on_crash ERR` armed — `on_crash` reports and returns 0, but under `set -Eeuo pipefail` the shell exits anyway, so `run_phase audit` never returned and `run_phase remediate` was never run and never reported, contradicting the comment directly above the cycle branch. The operator saw one `CRASHED` comment and then silence, indistinguishable from the remediate phase hanging. The call is now `if ! ground_truth; then` with its own report and `return 0`, and the cycle branch wraps both phases in `set +e`. `testing_weekend.sh` also gained `fetch_origin_with_retry` (3 × 60 s) — it was still a bare single `git fetch` while reliability got the retry in 3f96dc31 for the 2026-08-23 port-22 blackhole, so it was the far more exposed of the two. (b) **R-238:** the cycle budget was tested at exactly one place, after `run_round` returned, so a check passing at `CYCLE_BUDGET_SECS - 1` still launched a further `CAP_SECS` — an effective 26 h against a 24 h `StartCalendarInterval`, and launchd drops the next fire for a running label with no record. Both the continuation check and a NEW check at the head of `run_phase` now require room for the round's own cap (`CYCLE_DEADLINE - CAP_SECS`); the head check matters because round 1 of each phase was unbudgeted, so a cycle already over budget in the audit phase still launched a full remediate round. The whole cycle is now bounded by `CYCLE_BUDGET_SECS`. (c) **R-239:** everything from `cd "$REPO"` to `run_phase` ran with no ERR trap — the marker check, `acquire_runner_lock`, `mkdir`, the rotation pipeline under `pipefail` — so a full disk, a moved clone or a held lock exited with nothing but a line on stderr. The trap is armed before the prologue in both loops, and both refusal branches now `report`. The lock branch names the recorded pid and says what to do, because `acquire_runner_lock` only reclaims when `kill -0` fails: a pid reused by any live unrelated process makes every subsequent daily fire exit 3 in under a second. (d) **R-240:** `~/.bun/bin` added to the testing plist's PATH — 3f96dc31 appended it to the reliability plist only, and the testing loop is the one whose entire remit is JS/vitest health; `setup_testing_weekend.sh` checks `bun` in the operator's INTERACTIVE shell, which resolves it, so setup printed ok and installed a plist under which it did not. A test now asserts both plists agree on PATH. (e) **R-266:** `WEEKEND_VENV` is literally the same path in both setup scripts and both wrappers prepend it to the running agent's PATH, so running either setup while the other loop's cycle was live re-created the interpreter and re-installed site-packages under a running agent. Each setup now checks the SIBLING clone's lock too; a test asserts the two setups still share a venv (the precondition) and that each guard exists. (f) **R-267:** log rotation had no exclusion list while the plists point `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` into that same directory — and it runs BEFORE `run_phase`, so the rest of the invocation wrote to a deleted inode launchd still held open. Both loops now exclude `launchd-cycle.log`/`.err`. **Regression caught and fixed during the work:** the new `grep -v` exits 1 when it filters everything out (an empty or sinks-only log dir) and `set -o pipefail` turned that into a prologue death — which the self-rewrite suite caught as `holder rc=1`. Guarded with `|| true`. **⚠ Operator action:** the two plist changes (`config/com.radon.testing-daily.plist` PATH) only take effect after `setup_testing_weekend.sh` reinstalls the job. Setup was deliberately NOT run from inside this cycle — it hard-resets the clone under the running agent, and the new sibling guard correctly refuses. Verified live: `launchctl list` shows both daily labels installed, `com.radon.reliability-daily` holding this run's pid. Full gates: pytest `8118 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest unchanged at `7564 passed`. |
| REL-090 (B) | DONE (R-241) | (this commit) | The coverage ratchet fails on an empty or partial map instead of reporting 100%. RED: `6 failed, 2 passed` in the new `scripts/tests/test_coverage_ratchet_fails_on_empty.py`. GREEN: `12 passed` with the existing merge suite. `_pct` returned `100.0` whenever `total <= 0` and `summarize` derives every total purely by iterating the merged map, so a single `coverage-final.json` of `{}` — or files carrying no `b` branch maps — produced `{"lines": 100.0, "functions": 100.0, "branches": 100.0}`, `evaluate` found no failures, and `main` printed `lines: 100.00% (gate 75%)` and returned 0. The only emptiness guard checked that at least one FILE existed, never that it contained statements, and this module's own docstring calls it "the only coverage ratchet in CI" — so the branch dimension could go permanently green on any reporter-shape change without a line of code changing. `_pct` now returns `None` for an absent dimension, `evaluate` treats that as a distinct failure ("no measurable total ... a broken report, not full coverage") rather than a threshold comparison, and `main` prints `n/a` instead of a number it does not have. Separately, `ci.yml` shards `--shard=N/8` while the merge job downloads with a `vitest-coverage-*` glob and this module globbed whatever landed, so a partial artifact download was gated as the whole suite: a new `--expect-shards` refuses a count mismatch and names what it found, and `ci.yml` passes `8`. A test asserts the workflow passes the flag at all, so the two cannot drift apart. Full gates: pytest `8127 passed, 1 skipped`; vitest unchanged at `7564 passed`. |

(f) **R-270:** Last Price divided by `pos.contracts * getMultiplier(pos)` with no zero guard on either the render path or the sort extractor, so a row flattened mid-sync reached `fmtPrice`'s `.toLocaleString()` as `Infinity`/`NaN` and printed `$∞`/`$NaN` while driving the up/down flash. A shared `finiteOrNull` covers both. **Pinned test rewritten:** `share-pnl.test.ts` asserted `data.pnl === 0` for a group whose `totalPnL` is null — that IS R-249, and the group in question is an OPENING one with no realized P&L at all. (d) **R-272:** `coneFillPct` returned `0` for both "cone bounds unavailable" and "sitting at or above the p90 ceiling", and the bar reads longer-is-better — so a candidate whose bounds failed to compute rendered a real IV number beside an empty bar that reads as maximally rich. (e) **R-273:** `finiteBrushValue` mapped a null `atm_iv` to `0` and fed the brush, plotting a missing session as a 0% implied-vol floor spike — while `VolConeSeriesPoint.atm_iv` is `number \| null` and its type comment says "Nulls are preserved for chart gaps", which the main chart honours. The brush is what the trader drags to pick the chart window, so a gap read as a crash-low print. Renamed `brushValue` and returns `null`. **Pinned test rewritten:** `scanner-hero-mapping.test.ts::"draws nothing when the cone is unusable"` asserted `.toBe(0)` — that IS R-272, the exact collision. Those three steps are now wrapped and write a `newsfeed-precycle` error row. (f) **R-262:** `createShutdown` exited 0 unconditionally after the grace, so a SIGTERM landing between `persistPosts` (disk) and `upsertPosts` (Turso) read to systemd's `Restart=on-failure` as a clean stop. It now takes an `isCycleInFlight` probe — threaded from `run()`, since the abort signal is only checked BETWEEN cycles and nothing else can tell — and exits 75 when it cuts a cycle in half. (g) **R-263:** `useOrders` had no visibility gate, so a backgrounded tab issued a `no-store` GET (a live Turso read) every 30 s through the night and the weekend when the open-order snapshot cannot change; and no backoff, so a 503 was retried at exactly 30 s forever. It now skips the fetch while hidden and walks a bounded ladder to a 5-minute ceiling, matching what `usePortfolio` already did. (h) **R-269:** the flow footer asserted `Dark Pool Lookback: 5 Trading Days` as a literal beside two payload-derived numbers, against the UI Copy Rules. | REL-094 (B) | DONE (R-274) | (this commit) | **P0 — the journal replay can no longer fabricate realized P&L through contract-identity corruption.** RED: `4 failed, 31 passed` in the new `TestContractIdentityCorruption` in `scripts/tests/test_journal_realized.py`; each corrupted-field case published the fabricated `{'c1': 4000.0}` against a true `$3,000`. GREEN: `35 passed`; journal-adjacent slice `614 passed` (`-k "journal or realized or basis or rehydrate or orders or execution_sweep"`). `_unusable_fill_key` treated EVERY `_bucket_key() is None` row as excluded-by-design, but that predicate conflates the three genuine by-design exclusions with a row that NAMES an option and fails normalisation (`right: ""`, `strike: null`, an unparseable `expiry`, or the field missing outright). Such a row is a fill the contract's history is missing; dropping it removes its quantity and its cost together, so both replay guards — which are quantity-only — cancel exactly and the close is priced against too small a basis. The fix tests the payload's own SHAPE (`_is_option_shaped`: a declared `sec_type`, else the BAG `right == "?"` marker, else whether any of `right`/`strike`/`expiry` is named at all) rather than the normaliser's return, and the `CLOSED` check moved ahead of `_bucket_key` so a rehydrated round trip is still excluded first. A corrupt row cannot name its own bucket, so it poisons by the widest scope it can still identify — `TICKER|*`, or `*|*` when even the ticker is gone — and `_is_unusable` resolves the wildcards at the consumer. Over-poisoning costs an IB fallback; under-poisoning fabricates a figure. The three by-design exclusions (BAG envelope, stock leg, rehydrated `CLOSED`) are pinned by direct `_unusable_fill_key(...) is None` assertions, and the pre-existing `test_bag_and_stock_rows_are_ignored` behaviour is re-asserted unchanged. Cross-ticker scoping pinned: a corrupt GLD row leaves SLV's figure intact. |

| REL-099 (B) | DONE (R-288, R-289, R-290) | (this commit) | **P1 — the CTA percentile guard becomes two-sided and stops trusting unverifiable rows.** RED: `7 failed, 2 passed` in the new `web/tests/cta-percentile-guard-symmetry.test.ts`. GREEN: `9 passed`; CTA slice `26 passed` (reconcile, route repair, briefing, freshness). (a) **R-288:** one flat `Z_DISAGREEMENT_LIMIT = 35` is ONE-SIDED, because rounding a fraction always lands on 0 or 1: rounding DOWN (0.30 → `0`) yields a gap of 30 and sails under the limit, while rounding UP (0.65 → `1`) yields 64 and is caught. The low half is exactly the half that inverts a narrative into "max short". The limit is now derived from the cell's SHAPE: a `percentile_3m` that is an integral value inside 0-1 carries the rounding signature and gets a tight 2-point limit (a genuine 0th/1st/100th percentile has a z-score that agrees to well under two points); anything else is a real integer percentile subject to ordinary extractor noise and keeps 35. Swept 0.03-0.45 with a z consistent with each: zero survivors, against all 43 surviving before. **Deviation from the acceptance criteria, stated:** the sweep starts at 0.03, not 0.00. A true fraction of 0.00-0.02 rounds to `0` and its z-score implies ~0-2, so it is indistinguishable from a genuine 0th percentile by ANY gap rule — and publishing 0 for it is correct, not a defect. (b) **R-289:** `implied == null` (z absent, `null`, `""` or `NaN`) made `contradicted` false, so the percentile was published unverified. It is now nulled and tracked as `unverifiable`, separately from `contradicted`. All four inputs pinned. Safe in practice: every `menthorq_cta` row on record carries a `z_score_3m`. (c) **R-290:** `fractional` required a non-integer in the trio, so an all-integral trio from a fractional card (`[0.0, 1.0, 0.0]`) read `1.0` as the 1st percentile instead of the 100th — opposite narratives. The z-score now breaks that tie by choosing whichever scale it agrees with. **Scoping clause of R-291 landed here** (same lines, so splitting it across two commits would have meant editing them twice): a contradiction on a row with the rounding signature means the whole TRIO was mis-scaled and all three columns go, but a gross disagreement on a real integer percentile is evidence about the 3M column only and no longer blanks `percentile_1m` / `percentile_1y` with it. The signature travels with the trio through the cross-table repair map, so a rounded cell repaired from an unrounded copy is judged on the ordinary limit rather than the tight one — without that, the two existing repair tests went red. |
