(a) **R-310:** the CTA plate derived its canvas height straight from the row count read out of a cache FILE — `50 + sections*64 + rows*28 + 20` — with no cap on either input. A 5,000-row extraction asks `ImageResponse` for a **140,000px** canvas on a Node-runtime satori render that has to hold the whole bitmap, and the route declared no `maxDuration`. The arithmetic moved to `lib/ctaImageLayout.ts` so it is testable without booting an ImageResponse: rows are capped at `MAX_IMAGE_ROWS` (400), sections at 12, the result clamped to `MAX_IMAGE_HEIGHT`, and non-finite/negative inputs floored — a normal payload's height is byte-identical (3 sections, 30 rows → 1102, pinned). The route TRUNCATES its tables against a row budget before laying out, so the plate renders truncated rather than attempting the layout. **Whole class checked, and it is one site:** the two sibling satori routes (`menthorq/[command]/image`, `share/pnl`) size their canvas from constants (500 / 630), so the unbounded-HEIGHT defect is the CTA route's alone. All three ran the same unbounded render, though, so all three now declare `maxDuration = 30` and the test asserts it across the set rather than at the one cited route. (b) **R-311:** `loadFonts` assigned `fontRegular` BEFORE reading the Bold face. When the Bold read rejected, the regular was already cached, so every later call skipped the load block entirely and returned a Bold entry with `data: null` — for the lifetime of the process, from one transient failure. Both faces are now read with `Promise.all` and the PAIR is assigned only on full success, so the failure is retryable. Pinned by calling the loader twice: the first rejects, and once the file returns the second call must produce a non-null Bold. A second case asserts a fully-successful load still caches (the second call issues no further reads), so the fix does not trade poisoning for a re-read on every share image. All three image routes share this loader. |
| REL-106 (B) | DONE (R-312, R-313) | (this commit) | **P1 — a long assistant turn is interruptible, and a proposal never carries a missing datum.** RED: `4 failed` in the new `web/tests/assistant-stream-and-proposal-guards.test.ts` (one of them a 5s TIMEOUT, which is the defect rendering itself). GREEN: `7 passed`; full web vitest `7358 passed` (727 files, one known load-flake). (a) **R-312:** `streamMessage` walked the whole reply in 120-char chunks with an 8ms sleep between each and no way to stop — a 400 KB reply is ~3,300 chunks and ~27 seconds of forced typing that CONTINUES after the panel unmounts, calling `setMessages` on every one. It now takes an `AbortSignal` and checks it before each write, and `MAX_STREAM_CHUNKS` (240) bounds the animation. Everything past the cap lands in ONE final write rather than being dropped, so the full text always renders and only the animation is bounded — pinned by asserting the rendered length equals the source length for a 400 KB reply. `ChatPanel` owns an `AbortController` per turn, aborted on unmount and superseded on a new send. **Assertion adjusted, and why:** the cap test first read `writes <= MAX_STREAM_CHUNKS` and measured 241. The extra write is the remainder write the full-text guarantee rests on, so the bound is `MAX_STREAM_CHUNKS + 1`, with a companion `> MAX_STREAM_CHUNKS` assertion so the cap cannot be quietly raised to make the test pass. Against ~3,300 unbounded writes the bound is the point. (b) **R-313:** `statementFor` guards `iv_rv_edge` with `Number.isFinite` and then interpolated `range_score` RAW one clause later, so a result missing it rendered the literal `"range score undefined"` in the sentence the operator sizes a trade against. `alternativesFrom` had the identical hole via `Math.round(row.score)` → `"SCORE NaN"`. Both guarded; the alternative falls back to the verdict, which is always present. A complete result renders byte-identically (pinned: statement contains the score, alternative reads `SCORE 77`). **Fixture note:** the first draft of the proposal cases passed vacuously — `buildScannerProposal` returns `null` unless `verdict === "THETA_HARVEST"` AND every entry in `gates` is truthy, and the fixture had neither. Corrected before trusting the red; the three cases then failed with exactly `range score undefined`, `NaN`, and `SCORE NaN`. |
| REL-107 (B) | DONE (R-305, R-306, R-307, R-308, R-309) | (this commit) | **P2 — the freshness rail earns its colour.** RED: `9 failed, 3 passed` in the new `web/tests/freshness-rail-calendar.test.ts`. GREEN: `33 passed` across all three rail suites; full web vitest `7372 passed` (728 files, one known load-flake). (a) **R-305, partial and stated:** `lastCompletedSessionDate` models a session as any WEEKDAY, so on a holiday evening the rail names a date the exchange will never print and every EOD panel goes amber waiting for it. `lastPrintedSessionDate` now walks back to a real trading day using `isUsTradingDay` — the holiday SoT the app already ships (`scripts/config/market_holidays.json`). Thanksgiving and Christmas pinned; a genuine missed weekday still goes behind. **The EARLY-CLOSE half is NOT implemented, and deliberately so:** the client has only the full-closure table, and early closes are derived server-side from IBKR liquid hours (`market_calendar.py:_load_ibkr_calendar`). Closing that needs an early-close table on the client — it is not something this function can infer, and inventing one would be a hand-maintained list that goes stale silently. Recorded in the code comment at the call site. (b) **R-306:** a null/empty `asOf` reported `behind: false` and rendered `"Current"` with a ticking countdown over a panel holding NO date — the one thing it cannot honestly claim. The rail now carries `unknown` and the component gives it its own `data-state`, anchor note and muted styling (muted, not amber: nothing is known to be LATE). **The pinned test named in the finding was rewritten, not deleted** — `renders without a data date` asserted `data-state === "current"`; it keeps its original intent (the rail renders, does not crash, shows `---`) on the corrected state. (c) **R-307:** the alarm fired the instant the slot passed. `WRITER_GRACE_MS` (60m) is the sum the finding names — `RandomizedDelaySec=120`, plus the job's runtime, plus the panel's hourly poll: until a poll has had a chance to land, the rail is looking at data fetched BEFORE the run. `behind` still shows immediately; only the ALARM waits. **Three existing tests pinned the false page** (all three asserted overdue at slot+20m); each was moved past the grace with its intent intact, and a new case pins the in-window state as behind-but-not-alarming. (d) **R-308:** `msOverdue` was `now - lastSampleAt`, capped at one interval, so a twenty-minute miss and a seven-day outage were indistinguishable. It is now measured from `expectedRunFor` — the first scheduled run that owed a reading newer than `asOf`. A week-old reading reports >24h; a two-hour miss still reports two hours. The anchor is the END of the held session's day, not noon: a session dated D is carried by D's own evening run, and anchoring at noon over-reported by a full interval. (e) **R-309:** both rail clocks rendered a bare `"Today 18:10"` next to an `asOf` that is an ET session date. `targetLabel` now appends the viewer's own zone abbreviation via `Intl.DateTimeFormat`. |

Backlog: `RELIABILITY_AUDIT.md` §Delta audit 2026-08-28 §Backlog. Order: P0, then P1, then P2.

**Runner baseline (before any change, at branch tip `8a7b28e4`):** pytest (repo root) `8384 passed, 1 skipped, 90 deselected` — the single `test_perf_twr_residuals.py` failure in the first baseline run was caused by this run's own in-flight REL-110 edit (the warning-code allowlist is an AST scan read at test time, not at collection), not by the tree. vitest `7880 passed` (780 files). |---|---|---|---|
| REL-109 | **PARTIAL — DONE for the plausibility half, BLOCKED for the phantom-bucket half (root cause below)** (R-320, R-377) | (this commit) | **P0 — contract corruption that NORMALISES must poison too.** RED: `7 failed, 10 passed` in `TestContractIdentityCorruption`; the four corruptions named in the finding were first reproduced in the lead context against the healthy triple's true `{'c1': 3000.0}` — `strike=0`, `strike='0'`, `strike=600` and `expiry='20260819'` each returned `{'c1': 4000.0}` with no warning. GREEN: `43 passed` in the file, `54 passed` with `test_journal_basis.py`. Three mechanisms shipped: (a) a new `_is_plausible_contract` gate — `strike > 0`, `right` in `C`/`P`, `expiry` a REAL calendar date — checked by BOTH `_journal_entry` and `_unusable_fill_key`, so a key that normalises to an impossible contract poisons `TICKER\|*` instead of minting its own bucket; (b) `_is_option_shaped` now tests field PRESENCE (`field in payload`) rather than truthiness of the stringified value, so a numeric `0` strike is no longer read as field-absent and routed to the stock exclusion; (c) the module header and `realized_pnl_by_exec_id` docstrings restated. **BLOCKED half, stated after three attempts:** `strike=600` and `expiry='20260819'` still return `{'c1': 4000.0}` and cannot be closed here. Both mint a well-formed key for a contract that COULD be listed, so from journal rows alone they are information-theoretically indistinguishable from a legitimate second position held on the same ticker — the account really can hold SLV C60 and SLV C600 at once. The proposed plausibility domain does not reach them (600 is a plausible strike; 20260819 is a real date after the fill date), and a cross-row heuristic (order-of-magnitude strike outlier vs the ticker's other contracts) would poison real wide positions on no evidence. Root-cause hypothesis: closing this needs an AUTHORITATIVE contract set — IB positions or contract details — and `realized_pnl_by_exec_id(rows)` receives only journal rows. The residual is now written into the module header naming the exact case and telling callers that hold such a set to cross-check. |

A second correction the drill forced: GNU coreutils 9.4 exits **137**, not 124, when the `-k` SIGKILL is what actually ended the child, so the round is classified from the wrapper's OWN clock (`SECONDS - round_start >= remain`) rather than from a non-portable exit code. **(c) R-385:** new `reground_for_continuation` clears `.git/index.lock` (the cap SIGTERMs claude mid-commit) and chains its three git calls; the call site is `if ! ... then report; break`, so a failure is reported instead of ending the run silently mid-backlog under `set -e` with the ERR trap disarmed. `ground_truth` clears the lock too. **(d) R-409:** `net_bounded()` wraps every `gh` and network `git` call at `RADON_WEEKEND_NET_TIMEOUT_SECS` (120s default) — including the dead-man channel itself, where a hung `gh issue comment` inside the crash handler wedged the wrapper while reporting its own death — and the fetch runs under `core.sshCommand` with `ConnectTimeout`/`ServerAliveInterval`/`ServerAliveCountMax`. **(e) R-410:** the page-suppression third argument is gone from the four TERMINAL reports (REFUSED x2, SKIPPED, GROUND TRUTH FAILED) and kept only on the interim `ROUND n ... continuing` report. `test_a_clone_without_the_marker_is_refused_and_reported` PINNED the old behaviour (`_pages(cfg) == 0`) and was rewritten to require the page, keeping the one-comment/REFUSED half. **(f) R-411:** an empty `pid` file is now treated as HELD, not stale — the create-then-write window used to skip the `kill -0` test entirely and hand a second cycle the same tree — and the pid is renamed into place so no reader sees it half-written; `release_runner_lock` returns early unless the recorded pid is still `$$`, so the first run's EXIT trap can no longer unlock the second run's tree. **(g) R-426:** `phase_status` takes a byte mark and greps only what the LAST invocation appended, so a round-1 ceiling message no longer makes a finished round 8 report TRUNCATED forever. Two test-harness stubs needed updating: the `timeout` stub had to consume `-k <secs>`, and `STUB_TIMEOUT_124` had to be scoped to the `claude` invocation now that the `gh` calls also run under `timeout`. **Every edit was written via a temp file plus `os.replace`, never in place, so the live run reading this file by byte offset was unaffected.** |

A bare `YYYY-MM-DD` is treated as a literal calendar day and never converted, so it cannot shift west of the exchange. Before the cutover a duplicate-only run is still tolerated. |

| REL-149 | DONE (R-429, R-430) | (this commit) | **P1 — a push to main deploys with no root SSH, and the notify units actually reach `READY=1` in a container.** RED: `1 failed` in `cloud/tests/test_app_runtime.py` (proxy contract) and the new `cloud/tests/test_sync_control_plane.py`. GREEN: `34 passed` / `13 passed` plus the full `cloud/tests` run. **(a) R-429:** REL-138 restored `Type=notify` on the relay/monitor drop-ins, but the container's PIDs live in `system.slice/docker-<id>.scope`, so systemd dropped every `READY=1` sent from inside the container even with `NotifyAccess=all`: `radon-relay.service: start operation timed out` at 16:45:55Z and 16:47:33Z on 2026-08-29, and the installed drop-in was hot-patched back to `Type=simple` by hand. `radon-app-runtime run` now spawns `notify-proxy` (python3 stdlib, a child of ExecStart and therefore inside the unit cgroup), bind-mounts the proxy's socket into the container as its `NOTIFY_SOCKET`, and forwards every datagram to systemd; the container never sees the systemd socket. A proxy that cannot bind refuses to launch the container (exit 71) instead of starting a unit that can only time out. **(b) R-430:** the hot-patch then failed every deploy at `[preflight] installed control-plane target drifted`, and a helper edit earlier the same day (b8541271) failed them at `incompatible with scripts/deploy-root-helper.sh` — both needed a human `bootstrap-control-plane.sh` over root SSH (also 2026-08-21). New sudoers verb `radon-deploy-root sync-control-plane`: root reads `cloud/` through the GitHub main tip commit (same trust anchor as `install-units`, never the radon-writable tree), extracts it under root-only `/var/lib/radon/deploy/`, and runs THAT tip's own bootstrap, which validates, installs atomically and rewrites the manifest; already-current is a no-op. The deploy job calls `cloud/scripts/sync-control-plane.sh` before `deploy.sh` (bootstrap needs the deploy lock deploy.sh holds), retries only exit 75, and always defers the verdict to deploy.sh's preflight. Prestage skips instead of failing on a not-ready bundle. **Operator: this SHA edits the helper and sudoers — the LAST manual `bootstrap-control-plane.sh`; after it, helper/sudoers/polkit/unit/drop-in edits and root hot-patches self-reconcile on the next green push.** |

No deploy had gone green since the runtime-container drop-ins went live (last green `4b332fd8`, 2026-08-28 17:47Z): every restart pulled a 4.8G `radon-node:<sha>` image after teardown and the ~60s HTTP gate failed on a container still downloading (`33265501795`, `33266517375`, both rolled back mid-pull), and the untouched per-SHA pairs (~5.8G each) filled the 75G disk on 2026-08-29 because `radon-disk-cleanup.timer` had never been enabled. `deploy.sh` now runs `prepull_app_images` between the staged build and `restart_services`: `sudo -n radon-app-runtime pull <sha>` (new sudoers glob `pull [0-9a-f]*`; the wrapper refuses anything but 40 hex chars) pulls exactly the pair `run` will resolve, then removes SHA-tagged pairs that are neither the target nor in use by a running container, so the previous release keeps its pair until the deploy after next and a rollback never pulls. Warn-and-proceed on any failure. |

## Weekend 2026-08-30 — delta remediation (REL-151…REL-167)

Backlog: `RELIABILITY_AUDIT.md` §Delta audit 2026-08-30 §Backlog. Order: P1, then P2. Nine worktree agents
grouped by subsystem (no two touching one file), each red/green on targeted suites; the lead re-verified
red by restoring the parent source, re-ran green, cherry-picked and pushed per task, and ran the full gates
sequentially at tranche boundaries (a sibling testing-weekend clone was running full suites concurrently).
**Baseline before any change (0e0f5a4e):** pytest `22 failed, 8973 passed, 1 skipped` — all 22 in the
flex-embargo family (`test_cash_flow_sync_flex_errors`, `test_cash_flow_sync_cadence`,
`test_corrupt_state_preserves_embargo`, `test_cash_flows_route_last_synced`,
`test_cash_flow_sync_timeout_retry_budget`) and environmental: this runner clone's `web/.env` (provisioned
by #168) carries `TURSO_DB_URL`, `cash_flow_sync.py`'s `load_dotenv` puts it in `os.environ` at import, and
`flex_embargo._durable_store_available()` then fails CLOSED when the pytest guard refuses the connection.
`TURSO_DB_URL= TURSO_AUTH_TOKEN= python3.13 -m pytest …` gives `24 passed` on the two worst files and matches
CI (green on the same SHA); every gate below is run that way. vitest must run from the REPO ROOT: from
`web/` the root-level `lib/tools/__tests__/ib-wrappers-db-source.test.ts` resolves via `process.cwd()` and
fails ENOENT (`1 failed, 8259 passed` from `web/`).

## NEW_FINDINGS

- **NF-7 (from REL-064 / R-155):** a PARTIAL close booked on expiry day still guards the residual, so the contracts that actually expired worthless are not swept. Writing a $0.00 expiration alongside a same-day close is the double-booking the guard exists to prevent, so resolving this needs a decision on how the sweep and a partial close reconcile — the same class of ambiguity REL-025 settled conservatively for round-trip rows. Pinned as current behaviour in `test_expiry_sweep_opening_fill.py`.

- **NF-4 (from REL-024):** `journal_rehydrate._group_key` has no date component, so ONE Flex bucket can aggregate a contract's fills across the whole pull window into a single composite-id row. REL-024's `(contract, date, signed qty)` fingerprint is 1:1 by construction and cannot match that row against the individual `executed_orders` fills inside it, so the exact-id and root layers are the only cover for aggregated buckets. A quantity-aggregating comparison (journal net vs `executed_orders` net per contract+date) would close it, but it needs a decision on how `CLOSED` round-trip rows contribute — the same ambiguity REL-025 resolved conservatively for the expiry sweep.

- **NF-3 (from REL-017):** relay stale-feed farm-down still does not gate the order UI's submit button (web-side half of R-008) — banner-only today.

- **NF-2 (from REL-008):** the daemon's unpaginated journal reads (`exit_orders.py` full-table SELECT, `journal_sync.py:263`) remain on the unbounded transport — the per-handler deadline now bounds the hang, but the Hrana-bounding rule (cursor pagination) still deserves its own pass.
- **NF-1 (from REL-005):** the documented Gate-3 2.5%-of-bankroll cap is still not enforced server-side — static qty/notional caps bound the fat-finger class, but a live net-liq-proportional check needs a cached snapshot read on the placement path (design decision: which snapshot, how stale is acceptable, fail-open vs fail-closed when unavailable). Follow-up, not chased mid-loop.

  branch, so a sidecar failure swallows a serving-path signal (R-398); `degraded` is accepted as
  recovery evidence for an off-box `aggregate_down` with no freshness gate on the local payload

  consumers read as a circuit-breaker embargo, so a permanently-failing cri-scan pages exactly once

| REL-189 | DONE (R-521, R-522, R-538, R-539) | (this commit) | **P1 — the credential store degrades per-row, binds its key, and radon-api always boots.** RED: `scripts/tests/test_rel189_credential_store_durability.py` (collection ImportError: `SecretKeyMismatchError` did not exist — nor did mint-refusal, key binding, sqlite conversion or atomic multi-field save) + `scripts/api/tests/test_rel189_credentials_routes_degraded.py` `4 failed` (a corrupt sibling row 500'd rotation of a healthy field; an unopenable DB was a raw 500 on PUT and GET; deleting the key file with rows present silently minted a new key). GREEN: both new files + `test_secret_store.py` + `test_credentials_routes.py` + registry/validators/no-leak `101 passed`. `secret_store.py`: `SecretKeyMismatchError`; `key_binding` fingerprint table (sha256 of the key; bound on first write only after proving the key decrypts an existing row); `_load_or_create_key` re-reads the key file before concluding it is gone (race loser reads the winner's key), refuses to mint over a DB holding rows (`_refuse_mint_over_existing_rows`), and catches the O_EXCL race; `set_secrets` writes every field in ONE transaction; `@_sqlite_guarded` converts `sqlite3.Error` to `SecretStoreError` on every public method; `busy_timeout=5000`. `routes/credentials.py`: `_open_store()` 503s (`CREDENTIAL_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`) on any `SecretStoreError` at construction; `_merged_values` treats an unreadable sibling as absent with a warning naming it; PUT stores-all-then-exports; DELETE and list wrapped; `bootstrap_exported_names` constructs the store inside the try and skips undecryptable rows per-name, naming each in the log. Pinned test rewritten, stated: `test_wrong_key_raises_integrity_error` now uses an EXISTING wrong key file — its old shape (missing second key path over a DB with rows) is exactly the R-522 mint-refusal path and now raises `SecretKeyMismatchError` at construction, covered by the new file. |
